MINISTRY OF COMMERCE 
(fllficc of the Chief Controller of Imports and Exports) 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 19th April, 1990 

S.O. 1721.—M/s, Hindustan Fertiliser Corporati. n Ltd., 
Namriip Unit. PO. Parbatpur Distt- Dibnigarh fAssam)— 
786623. were granted an Import 1 icence No. P/D/2276834, 
dated 22-5-1989 for Rs. 43,13,000 (Rupees Forty three lakhs 
and thirteen Thousand only) (Equivalent to DM. 5.10,400) 
for import of Emergency Spares under Italy. 

2. The firm has applied for issue of duplicate Exchange 
Control copy of the above mentioned licence which 
has been lo.st/misplaced by them, It has further been stated 
that Exchange Control copy of the licence has been lost 
without having been registered with any Rank/eustoms autho¬ 
rity and is totally unutilised. 

3. In support of their contention, ihr licensee has filed am 
affidavit on stamped paper duly sworn in before a notary 
public of U.T. of Delhi. I am accordingly satisfied and in 
exercise of the power conferred under Sub-Clause 9(CC) of 
the Iiiyort (Control) Order. 1955 dated 7-12-1985 as amended

that the said original Exchange Control Copy No. P/D/ 
2276834, dated 22-5-1989 issued to the M/s. Hiiidiislan IFer- 
tilizer Corporation, Ltd, is hereby cancelled. 

4 . A duplicate Exchange Control Copy of the said licence 
issued to the party accordingly.

Disputes Act, 1947 (,14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby published the foBowing award of the Centred Gov^ 
ernment Industrial Tribunal cum Labour Court, Jabalpur 
as shown in the Aunexure in the industrial dispute between the 
cinplo>ees m relation to the management of State Bank of

traj Government on 24-3-90,

PARTIES: 

Employers in relation to the State Bank of India, Jabal¬ 
pur and its workman. Shri Hanuman Prasad Agar- 
wal, Ex-Messenger, 412 Anand Kunj, Geraha, 
.Tabalpur (M.P.)

This is a reference made by the Ccrvtrnl Government. 
Ministry of Labour, vide its Notification No. L-12012/373/ 
86-D.lIlA) datd 25-9-1987, for adjudication of the follow¬ 
ing dispute:— 

"Whether the action of the management of State Bank 
ol India Jiibiilpiir in terminating services of Shri 
Hanuman Prasad Agarwal lix-Mes^engpr w.e.f. 
23-1-83 is justified? If not, to what relief lire con¬ 
cerned workman entitled?" 

2, Undisputed facts of the case arc that the workman. 
Ilunuman Prasad Agarwal S/o Shri Suraj Prasad Agarwal, 
was einpolyed at Garha Branch of State Bank of India and 
performed duties of Mcsscnger-cum-Furrash. He worked from 
3-5-1982 to 23-1-1983, His services were terminated. It 
has not licen cbalicngcd that S/Shri Satish Kumar Pathafc 
Bajciiclra Kumar and Kaniu weia engaged one after the ether 
after he was discontinued from service. 

3. The case of the workman in brief is that the workman 

I'crlormed duties of a Messenger-cum-Furrash with effect 
from 3-5-1982 and he continued to serve in this capacity 
upto 23-1-1983, Thereafter he was not permitted to con¬ 
tinue in service despite his repeated requests, and the man¬ 
agement ccinliniied to engage fresh hands in gross disregard 
to the principles of natural justice and contrary to the 
provisions of Industrial Disputes Act. Sastri Award and the 
Bipartite Agreement and Settlement. Thus the management 
kiiowlingly violated iJie term no, 3 of the Settlement dated 
15-2-85 which clarly .stipulates that all such of the employees 
'who had worked for 90 days or more wiJutS only be offer¬ 
ed employment. But, in the instant case, the management, 
instead of offering the employment to the workman con¬ 
cerned, emplpoyed other persons. The workman has been 
estopped from work by the management not on account of 
liny fault on his part but bcanse of the action of the man- 
ai'erncnf who have caused deliberate breaks with an ulterior 
motive to deprive the workman of the benelils of perma¬ 
nency which amounts to an unfair labour practieo. Thus 
the workman Is entitled to get relief of continuity of ser¬ 
vice within the meaning of Section 33B of the I.D. Act 
because the management had deliberately discontinued ser- 
s'ice from time to time and the workman is not responsible 
fur such forced ce sation of work. It amounts to illegal re¬ 
trenchment as per Sec, 2(oo) read with Sec. 25R Of the 
i n. Act. Workman is, therefore, entitled to all the benefits 
of continuity of service with full back wages and ancdlary 
reliefs such as leave, medical aid, leave encashment, bonus 
as per terms and conditions of service of sub-st^ emnlcv- 
ed by the Bank, ‘ '

THE GAZETTE OF INDIA ; JUNE 23. 1990/ASADHA 2, 1912 [Part II—Sfic. 3(ii)] 


4. According to the management, the workman was emp¬ 
loyed as purely temporary messenger-cum-furrash on daily 
wages from August 1982 tn March, 1983. During this 
period he only worked for a total period of 96 days as a 
Messenger-cum-h'urrnsh. He was also employed from May, 
1982 to July. 1982 on daily wages for purposes of sprinkl¬ 
ing water of khas tatties. In the month of April, 1983 he 
was again employed for the same job. Thus the workman 
worked as Messenger-cum-Furrash for 96 days and as khas 
tatties man for 64 days during the aforesaid period. The 
details of his employment in the said two capacities are as

5. The workman is found to have worked in the Canteen 
for 66 days during September, 1982 to April. 1983 as Can¬ 
teen Boy. The Canteen bemg not under the management of 
the State Bank of India, those days cannot be counted as 
the working period under the management of State Bank of 
India, S.nce he was on daily wages, he had no legal status 
to continue in the employment of the Bank and he was 
accordingly put off the employment. 

6. However, in terms of the Settlement reached with the 
Federation dated 15-2-1985 the workman was given a chance 
to face a regular selection. He appeared before the Selec¬ 
tion Board on 28-4-1986 but failed in the interview and 
he was accordingly advised. Thus his services could not 
be continued and came to an end, 

7. Tho management has denied that the workman was 
employed or had been performing the duties of Messenger- 
cumr-iFunash with effect from 3-5-1982 and continued till 
23-1-1983. He was kept on Ks. 5 per day on daily wages 
for khas laities purpose and during May to July, 1982 he 
worked aS khas tatti wala. He never worked as Messenger 
during the aforesaid period. The workman did not at all 
Work in Januaiy, 1983. He however worked for 10 days 
in Fehruray and for 13 days in March, 1983. He was given 
an opportunity for regular employment and was given a 
chance to appear before the Selection Board but he did net 
succeed and therefore it cannot be said that he was not 
given an opportunity for employment. The management 
did not violate the terms of settlement. It has been denied 
that he was deliboiately denied the work. Management was 
free to discontinue his services because he was a daily 
wager The conli'uct therefore commences in the morning 
and terminates in the evening. No provisiorcs of law have 
been violated. He has not completed 240 days of continu¬ 
ous service at any time and is not entitled to the protection 
under the I.D, Act. The reference is, therefore, liable to be 
rejected. 

8, The rnanagement has examined himmself in support of 
its case while the workman has examined himself. 

9, It may be pointed out that according to the workman 
he worked tipto 23-1-1983 while according to the manage¬ 
ment the workman worked from 'February, 1982 to April, 
1983 as detailed In the above Schedule. 

10, It Is true that though the workman has denied that ho 
Was a daily wage earner hut from his own statement It can be 
very well gathered that he was employed on dally wages 
'from time to time. There is no evidence on record to 
show that his services were deliberately discontinued by the 
management to defeat the provisions of law. Thuj it can¬ 
not be said that-he was illegally discontinued from service. 


But it is not a disputed fact that certain persons were emp¬ 
loyed in his place fiom time to time by the mauagement 
after his services were discontinued. According to the man- 
fsigement, the applicant workman could not be taken in ser¬ 
vice because he was asked to face the interview but he could 
not succeed and therefore his services could not be 
continued. 

11. From the pleading.s of the management it is evident 
that the applicant workman was to be given a chance for 
regular selection in terms of the settlement leachcd with 
the Federation dated 15-2-1985 (para 3). 

12. There Is, however, no evidence on reeprd that the 
workman was given opportunity to face the regular selec- 
ITon and he appeared before the Selection Board on 
28^1986 and because ho failed in the interview his services 
were discontinued, Nor even a question was suggested to 
the workman in this regard in his crosB-examination, That 
being so, it can be said from the. pleadings of the manage¬ 
ment itself that there has been violation of the terms of the 
aforesaid Settlement, That being so, stoppage of his ser¬ 
vices eithei from 23-1-1983 cr since Apiil, 1983 as pleaded 
by the management is not in accordance with the settlement 
arrived at between the parties and therefore the applicant 
workman is entitled to regular appointment in the cadre to 
which he is entitled from this date with no consaqiiomial 
ifelief. 

13. I need not go into other aspects of the case and as 
auch the reference is answered as follows :— 

The action of the management of State BE.nk of India. 
Jabalpur in terminating services of Shri Hanuman 
Prasad Agarwal. Ex. Messenger w.e.f. 23-1-1983 or 
since March, 1983 as pleaded by the nw-nagement 
is not justified, He is entitled to reinstatement in 
service tp the cadre tq which he was entitled after 
selection as pleaded from the date of (\is award. 
He is not entitled to any other relief. No order as 
Id Costs.

AWARD 

The industrial dispute between the above parties w«» 
referred td this Court for adjudication by the Oo^mment 


ie90/<lTT:T36, 1911 2973 


of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi as per Order No, 
L-12012/600/86-D,n (A) dated 4-11-1987. The issue 

covered by the reference is as follows ••—■ 

"Whether the action of the management of the State 
Bank of India in imposing the punishment of 
dismissal on Sri E. Vijayachandran, Head Clerk, 
Parappanangadi branch w.e.f. 22-4-85 is justified ? 
II not. to what relief is the workman concerned 
entitled ?” 

II. The dismissal was after a domestic enquiry. The 
validity of the domestic eiiquity was tried by fne as a preli¬ 
minary issue- I have found as per my order dated 
26-12-1989 that the domestic enquiry was not proper. That 
order contains necessary facts. So it is extracted here¬ 
under in full :— 

"PRELIMINARY ORDER 

The issue referred for adjudication to this Court by the 
Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for 
adjudication is the following ;— 

"Whether the action of the management of the State 
Bank of India in impioslng the punishment of dis¬ 
missal of Sri p. Vijayachandran, Head Clerk, 
Parappanangadi branch w.e.f, 22-4-85 is justified 7 
If not, to what relief is the workman concerned 
entitled 7" 

2. The workman has filed a claim statement stating as 
following ; 

The Workman had been dismissed from service by or'Ser 
dated 9-2-85, The punishment was shockingly dispropor¬ 
tionate to the nature of allegations. Even since his suspen¬ 
sion from 19-10-1983, the workman is out of eroployment. 
Discipline proceedings were purportedly initiated against 
him under ptilffigraph 521 of the Sastry Award, There were 
hve allegations raised against him. The first three related 
to the alleged nr^suse of his savj\gs bank account. The 
fourth charge related to deletion of an entry in the register. 
The fifth charge related to issuance of a cheque. By way 
of his reply dated 23-12-83, the workman hud e.xplained in 
detail the circumstances in v.nich the incidents occurred. On

employee and he has not requested the Bank to discount

it has to be held that the Management has not succeeded 
in proving that the delinquent has committed the misconduct 
of misappropriation. Therefore 1 find that the delinquent 
workman is not guilty of charge No, 2. 

VI. Charge No. 3 is 'h.it .S’hri Vijaynchandran has cursed

guihv of chiircc No. t 

VTI, Charge No. 4 and 5 are admitted by the delinquent 
on factual position. But it was contended that it was done

go to show that the delinquent has not made remarks against

of the Manager, There is no evidence in this case to show

has deleted the entrv under the authentication in cood 
faith and with bonnfide intention. Therefore I hold that 
charge No. 4 is also not a misconduct committed by -he 
deliuquent, 

VIIT. Tn the light of the above discussion T bold that 
delinquent is not guilty of charge Nos. 2, 3 and 4, hut be 
is guilty of charge Nos, ] and 5. 

IX. The question now remains tp be considered is regard¬ 
ing the propriety and legality of the punishment of dismissal 
imposed on the delinquent workman. The learned counsel 
for the workman would argue that the punishment of 
dismissal imposed on the delinquent is disproporliouaie to 
the gravitv of the misconduct and therefore the order of 
dismissal from service cannot he justified. But the learned 
eoniisel for the Management would argue that the misconduct 
I'onimifleed by the delinquent deserves the punishment of 
dismissal and therefore no interference in the piiiushment 
by the court under Sec. 11-A of the l.D, Act is called for. 
But it is pertinent to note that it is an established principle 
of industrial law that the puni.shment .should he commen¬ 
surable with the mi.sconducts proved. The misconducts prov- 
cd in this cnsc are that he had issued cheques for the amount 
in exces.s of balance amount in his account. Thii cannot he 
considered to be a serious misconduct w,ariaPting the 
punishment of dismissal from service. Taking into considera¬ 
tion the entire facts and attendant circumstances of the case 
I am of opinion that Justice will be met if the workmen 
Is reinstated in service without bock wiyvcs but witli fon- 
tmuity of service. 

X. In the result an award is passed directing the Mnnape- 
ment to reinstate the workman Shri F., Vllavaclinndran in 
service without hacf- wngev hut uith conkniiity of service.

■fhe Central Government has referred the following Indus-,

2. Shri C. K. Mahadevan, the workman involved in the 
present matter (for short, 'the workman’) is represented by 
the Regional Secretary of the Air Corporation Employees’ 
Union, Santacruz, The workman on release from the Air 
Force after 24 years of service was employed by the Indian 
Airlines (hereafter for short, ‘the Management’) with effect 
from 12th February, 1979 as Plant Technician in their 
Ground Support department. He was confirmed n service 
with effect from i2th August, 1979. He was issued a 
charge sheet dated 16/23 July, 1984 for alleged misconduct 
a.i defined in para 28(13) and (15) of the Stcnding Orders. 
The first item relates to absence without leave or overstay¬ 
ing sanctioned leave without sufficient grounds or proper 
or satisfactory explanation. The second item is habitual 
breach of any standing order or any law or rules applicable 
to the Establi.shment. It was alleged that the workman 
was absent unauthorised'y for 206 days in the year 1982. 
for 146 days in 1983, two days in June, 1984 and again 
continuously from 2nd July, 1984 onwards. Shri L, S. 
Verma, Assistant Manager, Perspnncl Services, Bombay 
conducted the enquiry and held both charges proved. The 
Deputy Conti'oller. Ground .Support who accord'ng fo the 
Munngernent is the conipcleni authority confirmed the 
findings and nfter issuing show cau'e notice to the wo'kman 
and consider-'ng lik rrnly, d-'smi'-’d 'rirn from 'C "ti-e by 
his order dated 17/24 January, 198,5. The workman’s 
appeal was d-’smissed bv (he Regional Director by an order 
dated llih March, 1985. 

3. The workman has raised two noinfs In his statement of 
claim. In the first place, he state.s that tinder the standing 
orders. It was the Manager of the fa-'tory alone and none 
else who had the power to dism'ss him. As such, the work¬ 
man asserts, the impugned order which is not passed by the 
Manager, is bad in law- He does not challenge the validity 
of the enquiry on any other ground. His second contention 
concerns the merits of (he decision. According to h'm, there 
were sufficient grounds for h^s absence without prior permis- 
sifin. in.-ismncU ns he wns not keep’nc good health. Unfortu¬ 
nately ho failed to produce evidence on this aspect dur’ng 
the course of the cnaniry. At such, ho submits, the autho¬ 
rities took a rcriciis view nn-l awgr-dcrl t|ie extreme renaltv 
of dismissal. He requests for settirig aside fhe order of dis- 
missat and regnlarisation of his abs'rice as leave that is 
admissible to him under various relevant provisions. 

4. According to the Management, the order of dismissal 
h passed bv the competent authority and there is absolutely 
no iiislification for any len’encv to the workman. Tn this 
connection then nnint out that even e.asU'cr the worktnan was 
awardetl the nnnishment of stopnage of two increments with 
iv'rmannnt effect for h.ah.'tnal absence without permission In 
>enrs f979 (o 1981 They denv that he is entitled to re-

7. 'the workman relies on S.O. No. 26lvi and 29(6; for 
his submission mat the order of dismissal can be issued only 
by the iMan,ager and not by any otlier authority. S.U. No. 
20 deals witn the subject of termination of service while 

h. O. No. 29 deals with the rules of conduct of disciplinary 
iction. A bare reading of these two htanding Orders toge¬ 
ther makes it clear that they deal with entirely different sub- 
iect-s. S.O. No. 29 deals with disciplinary action; S.O. 26 
deals with termination of service simpliciter. The prefent 
workman s ca.se falls under S.O. 29 and not S.O. 26. Refe.- 
ence to S.O. 26 .s, (l-.eiclore, nut relevant. 

8 . S.O. 2.9i,6) no doubt provides inter alia that in avvardins 
runishment, the Manager shall take into account the gravity 
of the misconduct, the previous record of the workman, if 
any, and other e.xtenuating and aggravating circumstances. 
From this, the workman desires the Tribunal to draw the 
inference that it is the Manager alone and no other authority 
shat can pass an order of dismissal. 1 do not agree. There 

i. s a .schedule at the end of the Standing Orders, w'hich de¬ 
tails the authorities competent to pass orders of punishments 
in di‘ciplinarj' proceedings and authorities to whom appeals 
against Ihe orders passed by the original authorities will 
fie. The original schedule was substituted by a fresh one 
under the Notification of 1st November, 1974. Admittedly 
it applies to Factory workers and ns such to the present 
workman. This schedule makes it clear that the Deputy 
Controller, (Jiound Support, is the disciplinary authority who 
is empowered to pass an order of dismissal and Regional 
Director is the appellate authority. Miss Seth for the Man¬ 
agement ha.; stated categorically that the post of Deputy 
Director, Giound Support, is equivalent to that of ‘Manager' 
.and that Regional Director is still a superior officer. The 
use of the term ‘Manager’ in S.O. 29(6) is perhaps not happy, 
as on the first flush, it gives the impression that ‘Manager’ 
alone is enjoined to take into account relevant aspects before 
deciding upon the quantum of punishment. Now if the sub¬ 
mission of the workman is accepted that the final order can 
be passed by the Manager alone, it will lead to some obvi¬ 
ously absurd consequences. The entire schedule drawn in de¬ 
tail will become redundant. Then while disciplinary proceed¬ 
ings will be conducted by officers of equal status as the 
Manager and appeals will be heard by still superior officers, 
the final orders will be in the hands of the Manager. It 
cannot be contemplated for a moment that such ab.si!rd results 
were intended by the corporation who have framed the Stand¬ 
ing Orders under section 45 of the Air Corporations Act, 
with previous approval of the Central Government, Obviously 
in ths context obtaining, the term ‘Manager’ used in S O. 
No. 29(6) PS loosely used for the term ‘competent authority’ 
tnd cannot be assigned the special meaning given to it in the 
definition ciause. 

9. 'I'he impugned order cannot be faulted on the ground 
.that it is not passed by a competent authority. I uphold the 
competence of the Deputy Controller. Ground Support, to 
pas.s it and of the Regional Director to hear the appeal from 
it. 


10. The validity of the impugned order is net cliallenged 
on any other ground. I now turn to the merits of the case. 
There is ranre than adequate evidence adduced in the enquiry 
proceedings to reach the finding of guilt of the workman 
on both charges. The enquiry proceedings are exhibited at 
Exh. M-3. The Management had examined Shri V. K. 
Nair who -was maintaining altdfldance record in respect of 
the ground support department. The evidence of this person

13. T!ie last question concerns the quantum of punishment. 
Takins into consideration all the circumstances of the case, 
including the fact that the workman had worked for 24 years 
in the Indian Air Force and had some domenic difficulties 
and is leqiiired to maintain a family of 5 members, I think 
the extreme penalty of dismissal is rather too harsh. T reduce 
ft to removal from service simpliciter.

justified ? If not. to what relief the workman 

is entitled ?” 


.':3, 2-1 912 -9(^3 


' '2. the case of the managn.ment, delai's apart, is 
as follows 

■j'hs conccincd workman, Kairiji Saw, was emp¬ 
loyed as Driver in Barora Area Office in November, 
ivST, On receipt of a report in regard to certain 
lictf, of misconduct allegedly committed by him, he 
was issued widi a chargesheet dated 11-11-Si. 

‘It has been reported to the management that on 
11-11-81 at about 12-30 p.m. infront of Barora oflice 
main gate, you stabbed Dhiilon, Senior Personnel 
Officer, Barora Area by ‘Bhujaii’ with an attempt to 
murder. In the scuffle he received severe dip cuts 
in his left palm and bleeded (bled) profusely.” 

J( has been staled in the chargesheet that the al¬ 
leged misconduct attracted clause (r) of Standing 
Order No. 170) of the Model Standing Order appli¬ 
cable to Coal Mining Industry. The concerned work¬ 
man submitted his explanation to the chargesheet, 
but that was not found satisfactory. Hence, the 
management' directed an enquiry to be made into the 
charges framed against him and Sri A. N. Yadav, 
-Manager, Madhuban Colliery was appointed Enquiry 
Officer. In the course of domestic enquiry the wit¬ 
nesses for the management were examined in pre¬ 
sence of the concerned workman and he was given 
fuh opportuniiy to cross-examine them. Fie availed 
himself of such opportunity; he, v/as given opportu¬ 
nity to mak'.e a s'alcment in support oc his defence 
which he did. He was given opportunity to produce 
witnesses In support of his defence, but he did not 
produce any such witness. On the basis of enquiry 
held by him, the Lirquiry Officer found the workman 
guilty of the charge framed against him and submit¬ 
ted his report accordingly. The report of the En¬ 
quiry Officer was considered by the management and 
final decision was taken by the Generaf Manager' 
Chief Mining Engineer, Barora Area to dismiss him 
from service. Accordingly, he was dismissed from 
'ervicc by order i'-stied by the General Manaperj 
Chief Mining Engineer, Barora Area on 20-4-83 with 
immediate effect. It is asserted that the domestic en¬ 
quiry was held in accordance with the principle c.f 
natural justice and all reasonable opportunities were 
provided to the concerned workman to defend hira- 
'eif. It has been submitted that regard being had to 
the gravity of misconduct proved against .the concer¬ 
ned vvorkman which was very serious in nature and 
the facN and circumstances of the case, the action 
taken to dismiss him from serv'c.e was proper and 
justified.

Jt appears that Shri Dhillon was one of tlic four

proval of the Committee for promotion, I hold that

U, To enable an employer to peaceful carry on 
his indnstrlal activities, the Industrial Disputes Act

mi 


conferred power on him to prescribe conditions of 
service including enumerating acts of misconduct 
when committed within the premises of the esta¬ 
blishment. Act of misconduct may take place with¬ 
in the premi.ses of the establishment or in the vis- 
inity thereto, in the present ease the alleged mis¬ 
conduct if believed took place within the vismity 
of the establishment. But there must be casual 
connection in order to provide linkage between 
the alleged act of misconduct and employment and 
that coimcction must be real and substantial imme¬ 
diate and proximate and not remote or tenuous. 
The nuinagcmcnl could not provide respouablc ba.sis 
for motive of the misconduct allegedly committed 
by the concerned workman. On the other hand, 
the concerned workman has suggested a motive 
which is unconnected with his employment. 
It appears that he complained to the Higher 
authonty against the conduct of Shii 
Dhillon for liis zeach of faith with regard 
to his prom'sc to pay the balance considera¬ 
tion^ amount for purchase of the vehicle {Ex. W-6). 
But the higher authority ignored this complaint 
(E.X. W-7) and held that both Shri Dhillon and the 
concerned workman were wrrong to enter into a 
deal. It i.s beyond comprehension as to bow a per¬ 
son can be considered to be wrong to enter into u 
deal with another person. Anyway, there is no evi¬ 
dence on record that there was casual connection 
to provide linkage between the alleged acts of mis¬ 
conduct and employment. 

12. It appears from the evidence that the Gov¬ 
ernment have since withdrawn the case against the 
concerned workman. This withdrawal has been 
made under section 321 of Cr. P.C, and in the 
interest of administration and justice. In other 
words the Government did not coasider that the 
complaint of Shri Dhillon was of such nature 
W'hich, if not pursued with result in failure of ad- 
mmistration ol justice. There is no evidence on 
record that the alleged misconduct of the concerned 
workman has otherwise aggravated industrial indis¬ 
cipline amongst the workman of Barora Area, or 
distrubed harmonious industrial relationship bet¬ 
ween the cmplnycr and employee of that Area. 

13. Considering all the facts and circumstances 
and evidence on record. I am constrained to hold that 
the management of Barora Area No. 1 of Mjs- 
B.C.C. Ltd. was not justified in dismiss'ing the con¬ 
cerned workman from service for his alleged mis¬ 
conduct. Consequently the concerned workman 
should be reinstated in service and in the ciriumstaners 
of the case, with .50% of back wntes till his reinstate¬ 
ment, 

14. Hence, the follow i.ng award is rendered—the 
action of the management of Barora Area No. 1 
of Ms. B.C.C- ltd. in dismiss'ing the concerned 
workman. Pnmii Saw from service with effect from 
20-4-83 was not proper and justified. The manage¬ 
ment is directed to reinstate him in .sers/ice with 
effect from the dale of his dismi sal with 50% of 
back wages from the period of his disrnissal from 
■senice till his reinstatement. Die management is 
als'n directed to reinstate him in service within one 
month from the date of publication of this award. 

In the circumstances of the case. 1 award no co'-r.

]css there is a very glaring and apparent

age determination Committee) Medical 
Board.

.“i. The decision of the Committee will be bind¬

ti. The above procedure will come into force

this subject. 

1 consider it imperative on the part of the man¬

management is directed to have the age of the con¬

cation of the award and so, the action of the manage¬

11, Hence, the following award is rendered.—The 
action of the management of South Govindpur Col¬ 
liery of M!s. Bharat Coking Coal Ltd. in not correct¬

man to be less than what hag been recorded in Form

atre of superannuation in the meantime. But if he has

19-.'i-108S till fhe date of publication of the award and 
comnletion of determination of age, then the manage¬ 
ment should pay him back wages and other benefits 
only. 

Tti the circumstances of the case. T award no cost.
